\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}}\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|^{2}+16\alpha\tau^{\prime}\|\theta_{t}-\theta^{*}\|^{2}+16\alpha\tau^{\prime}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\mathchoice{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}$}}}}{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}$}}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2 \alpha\tau'}}}\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|^{2}+16\alpha\tau^{\prime}d_{t}+16\alpha\tau^{\prime}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}O(\alpha\tau^{\prime})d_{t}+O(\alpha\tau^{\prime})K\sigma^{2}.

Here, for (a), we used
\max\{\|A_{t}\|,\|\bar{A}\|\}\leq 2
; for (b), we used
\|\theta^{*}\|\leq\sigma
; and for (c), we used Lemma
8
.

Bounding
T_{3}
.
To bound
T_{3}
, we proceed as follows:

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\|A_{t}\|\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|

(53)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\tau^{\prime}\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|^{2}+\mathchoice{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}}}{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}}\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\alpha\tau^{\prime}d_{t}+\left(2\alpha\tau^{\prime}+\mathchoice{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}}}{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}$}}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{\alpha\tau'}}}\right)\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O(\alpha\tau^{\prime})d_{t}+O(\alpha\tau^{\prime})K\sigma^{2},

where in the last step, we used Lemma
8
and
\alpha\tau^{\prime}\leq 1.
The term
T_{4}
can be controlled in exactly the same way as above, with the same resulting bound.

Bounding
T_{2}
.
To bound
T_{2}
, we will invoke mixing properties of the underlying Markov chain as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[T_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*},(A_{t}-\bar{A})\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*},(A_{t}-\bar{A})\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\rangle|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}},X_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*},\mathbb{E}\left[(A_{t}-\bar{A})\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}},X_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\right]\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*},\left(\mathbb{E}\left[A_{t}|X_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\right]-\bar{A}\right)\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\|\left(\mathbb{E}\left[A_{t}|X_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\right]-\bar{A}\right)\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\|\mathbb{E}\left[A_{t}|X_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\right]-\bar{A}\|\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\alpha\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\left(\|\theta^{*}\|+\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\left(\sigma+\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq O(\alpha)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|^{2}+\sigma^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq O(\alpha)\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta_{t}-\theta_{t-\tau^{\prime}}\|^{2}+d_{t}+\sigma^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}O(\alpha)d_{t}+O(\alpha K\sigma^{2}).

In the above steps, (a) follows from the definition of the mixing time
\tau^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}_{mix}(\alpha)
in Definition
1
, and (b) follows from Lemma
8
and
\alpha\tau^{\prime}\leq 1.
Combining the bounds on
T_{1}-T_{4}
leads to the claim of the lemma.
∎

We now have all the ingredients needed to complete the proof of Theorem
3
.

To establish the claim, it remains to argue that
\mathbb{E}[d_{\bar{T}+\tau^{\prime}}]\leq O(K\sigma^{2}).
To that end, using the same reasoning as we did to arrive at Eq. (
49
), we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{\bar{T}+\tau^{\prime}}\|\leq 2\|\theta_{\bar{T}}\|+O(\sqrt{K}\sigma)

\displaystyle=2\|\theta_{0}\|+O(\sqrt{K}\sigma)

(57)

\displaystyle=O(\sqrt{K}\sigma).

Here, we used that for
t\leq\bar{T},\theta_{t}=\theta_{0}
, and
\|\theta_{0}\|\leq\sigma.
Thus,
d_{\bar{T}+\tau^{\prime}}=\|\theta_{\bar{T}+\tau^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}\|^{2}\leq 2\|\theta_{\bar{T}+\tau^{\prime}}\|^{2}+2\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}\leq O(K\sigma^{2}).
Combining all the pieces together, we have

Appendix F
Proof of Lower Bound in Theorem
4

In this section, we will establish the lower bound in Theorem
4
. Let us start by explaining the high-level idea behind our proof, and then we will supply all the technical details.

The main idea is to construct two different Markov Reward Processes (MRPs) induced by the same policy, such that (i) the value functions induced by the policy differ in magnitude by
\Omega(\sqrt{\epsilon})
in the two MRPs; and (ii) the distribution of rewards under the Huber-contaminated observation model is identical across the two MRPs. It is then not too hard to argue that any estimator for a value function must suffer an error of
\Omega(\sqrt{\epsilon})
on at least one of the two MRPs. We now proceed to construct our hard instance.

Step 1: Construction of the MRPs.
Consider a MDP with just one state
s
and one action
a
. Trivially, a policy
\mu
thus maps
s
to
a
, and there is no randomness in terms of state transitions. We will now construct two MRPs, MRP 1 and MRP 2, induced by the policy
\mu
, that differ in terms of their noisy reward models. For MRP 1, the reward random variable
r_{1}(s)
has support comprising two values:

In light of the above facts, we now proceed to argue that any value-function estimator must suffer
\Omega\left(\mathchoice{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise error in at least one of the MRPs.

Step 3. Lower-bounding Error of any Estimator.
For
i=1,\ldots,T
, let
(X_{i},Y_{i})
be independent pairs of random observations satisfying:

\mathbb{P}(X_{i}=Y_{i}=-\rho/{\sqrt{\epsilon}})=\mathchoice{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}}}{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}},

\,\mathbb{P}(X_{i}=Y_{i}=0)=1-\epsilon\,,\,\mathbb{P}(X_{i}=Y_{i}=\rho/{\sqrt{\epsilon}})=\mathchoice{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}}}{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}$}}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\epsilon}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\epsilon}{2}}}.

Let us note that
X_{i}
is distributed as per
(1-\epsilon)\mathcal{D}_{1}+\epsilon\mathcal{Q}_{1}
, and
Y_{i}
as per
(1-\epsilon)\mathcal{D}_{2}+\epsilon\mathcal{Q}_{2}
. Clearly, the following is true:
\mathbb{P}\left(\{X_{i}\}_{i\in[T]}=\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in[T]}\right)=1.
Now suppose
\hat{R}_{T}
is any estimator for estimating the means of the rewards in the two MRPs. As we shall see, establishing a fundamental limit on the performance of
\hat{R}_{T}
is sufficient to establish a limit on the performance of any value-function estimator. In what follows, for conciseness of notation, let

B\triangleq\mathchoice{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 (1-\epsilon)}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 (1-\epsilon)}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 (1-\epsilon)}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 (1-\epsilon)}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 (1-\epsilon)}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 (1-\epsilon)}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 (1-\epsilon)}}$}}{\raise (1-\epsilon)}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 (1-\epsilon)}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{\rho\sqrt{\epsilon}}{ 4 . Using

0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}$}}}}{\mathord{\mathchoice{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}$}}{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}$}}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}}{\mathchoice{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{0}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{1}{1}{2}}{\genfrac{}{}{}{2}{1}{2}}}.

(70)

This completes the claim and the proof.

Appendix G
Simulation Study

In this section, we report some synthetic experiments to support the theory developed in this paper. All simulations are performed on an HP Spectre x
360
personal laptop with
11
th Gen Intel(R)
4
-Core Processor.

Basic Setup.
We construct an MDP with
100
states, and use a feature matrix
\Phi
with
K=10
independent basis vectors. Using this MDP, we generate the state transition matrix
P_{\mu}
and reward vector
R_{\mu}
associated with a fixed policy
\mu
. For all our simulations, the discount factor
\gamma
is set to
0.5
, and the rewards are generated uniformly at random from the interval
(0,5)
. Unless specified, the step size
\alpha
is chosen to be
0.1
. We perform
10
independent trials per simulation and average the errors from each trial to report the mean-square error
E_{t}=\|\theta_{T}-\theta^{*}\|^{2}_{2}
. With this basic setup, we now report various experiments below.

1.

Vulnerability of
TD
(0)
. The purpose of the first simulation is to reveal the vulnerability of the basic
TD
(0) algorithm to adversarial reward contamination. To that end, we consider a scenario where the corruption fraction is
\epsilon=0.001
, and the rewards are noiseless. In each state
s
, with probability
\epsilon
, the adversary injects a biasing signal of magnitude
100/\epsilon.
The outcome of this experiment is reported in Fig.
2
. When the step size is held constant at
\alpha=0.1
, convergence is to a ball centered around a perturbed parameter
\tilde{\theta}^{*}
, where
\tilde{\theta}^{*}
is as in Theorem
1
. The size of this ball scales with the effective reward magnitude that depends on the bias
100/\epsilon
. Hence, in the left display of Fig.
2
, we see large oscillations that depend on the bias magnitude. With a diminishing step-size of the form
\alpha_{t}=1/t
, Theorem
1
suggests exact convergence to the perturbed point
\tilde{\theta}^{*}
. This is reflected in the right display of Fig.
2
, where the mean-square error (MSE) converges to a steady-state value that is bounded far away from
0
.

2.

Performance of
Robust-TD
. In our next simulation, we assess the performance of
Robust-TD
. For our first experiment, the noise model comprises a zero-mean Gaussian distribution with a variance of 1. We consider the same biasing attack as before, where the attacker injects a constant bias of
100/\epsilon
. We vary the corruption probability
\epsilon
, and report our findings in the left display of Fig.
3
. For each value of
\epsilon
, the MSE converges to a small ball around
0
. In the next experiment, we fix the corruption probability to
0.01
and vary the noise variance level. We consider three different values of variance:
5
,
2.5
, and
1
. As expected, with a constant step size, the MSE settles down to a ball around the origin, where the size of the ball depends on the noise variance. Notably, complying with Theorem
3
, the MSE of
Robust-TD
is unaffected by the magnitude of the adversarial bias input.
